=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a tree with N vertices numbered 1 to N. The i-th edge connects vertices A_i and B_i.
Consider a tree that can be obtained by removing some (possibly zero) edges and vertices from this graph. Find the minimum number of vertices in such a tree that includes all of K specified vertices V_1,\ldots,V_K.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N K
A_1 B_1
\vdots
A_{N-1} B_{N-1}
V_1 \ldots V_K

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq K \leq N \leq 2\times 10^5
- 1 \leq A_i,B_i \leq N
- 1 \leq V_1 < V_2 < \ldots < V_K \leq N
- The given graph is a tree.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

7 3
1 2
1 3
2 4
2 5
3 6
3 7
1 3 5

Sample Output 1

4

The given tree is shown on the left in the figure below. The tree with the minimum number of vertices that includes all of vertices 1,3,5 is shown on the right.

Sample Input 2

4 4
3 1
1 4
2 1
1 2 3 4

Sample Output 2

4

Sample Input 3

5 1
1 4
2 3
5 2
1 2
1

Sample Output 3

1


Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    it = iter(data)
    N = next(it); K = next(it)
    adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]
    for _ in range(N-1):
        a = next(it); b = next(it)
        adj[a].append(b)
        adj[b].append(a)
    is_terminal = [False]*(N+1)
    for _ in range(K):
        v = next(it)
        is_terminal[v] = True
    # maybe del data? But it holds ints referenced by adj? If we del data, adj entries still reference int objects; terminal ints not referenced; okay. But `it` still references data? If we delete data but it exists? Actually it is list_iterator referencing data list; if we don't delete it, data remains. We can `del data, it` after reading. But if adj entries reference int objects from data, deleting data list doesn't delete int objects because adj entries reference them. It frees list and terminal ints. Good.
    # However if we use `data = list(map(int,...))`, adj.append(b) stores int object b from data. So okay.
    parent = [0]*(N+1)
    parent[1] = -1
    order = [1]
    for v in order:
        for to in adj[v]:
            if to == parent[v]:
                continue
            parent[to] = v
            order.append(to)
    cnt = [0]*(N+1)
    for v in range(1, N+1):
        if is_terminal[v]:
            cnt[v] = 1
    for v in reversed(order):
        p = parent[v]
        if p > 0:
            cnt[p] += cnt[v]
    edges = 0
    for v in range(2, N+1): # Wait parent[1]=-1, but nodes not necessarily numbered root? Root 1, so all other nodes have parent >0. But if N=1 range(2,2) empty. Good.
        c = cnt[v]
        if 0 < c < K:
            edges += 1
    print(edges + 1)

if __name__ == "__main__": main()

Let's prove edge criterion: For any edge e, removing it partitions vertices into S and T. If both S and T contain at least one specified vertex, then any connected subgraph containing all specified vertices must include e, because otherwise it would be contained entirely in one component? More precisely, if e not included, the subgraph's vertices are subset of one of the components? Since original tree minus e has two components; without e, a connected subgraph cannot contain vertices from both components because no other path. Thus to connect terminals on both sides, e must be included. If one side has no terminals, e is not needed: all terminals lie in one component, and minimal subtree can be found within that component; e not in union of paths between terminals. So exactly.

Let's revise code:
data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
it = iter(data)
N = next(it); K = next(it)
adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]
for _ in range(N-1):
    a = next(it); b = next(it)
    adj[a].append(b); adj[b].append(a)
cnt = [0]*(N+1)
for _ in range(K):
    v = next(it)
    cnt[v] = 1
del data, it
parent = [0]*(N+1)
parent[1] = -1
order = [1]
for v in order:
    for to in adj[v]:
        if to == parent[v]:
            continue
        parent[to] = v
        order.append(to)
for v in reversed(order):
    p = parent[v]
    if p > 0:
        cnt[p] += cnt[v]
edges = 0
for v in range(2, N+1):
    c = cnt[v]
    if 0 < c < K:
        edges += 1
print(edges + 1)

Code:
def main():
    import sys
    it = iter(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = next(it)
    K = next(it)
    adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]
    for _ in range(N-1):
        a = next(it)
        b = next(it)
        adj[a].append(b)
        adj[b].append(a)
    cnt = [0]*(N+1)
    for _ in range(K):
        v = next(it)
        cnt[v] = 1
    del it
    ...
